It's far, far more than I would ever serve myself at a normal meal.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, it's been pretty consistent. I'd say there's hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. And then, of course, I have to try and make up for it right after, whether that's by throwing up, just not eating the rest of the day, or pushing myself way too hard on my daily walks. It's at least a weekly thing, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be attractive if you gained a few pounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I would be attractive at all. It actually terrifies me. Even though my daughter and a woman from my church have both told me recently that I look too thin, I just... I can't see it that way. I still panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug, or if the scale goes up by just one pound. It feels like I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, and I just can't stand the thought of gaining any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I suppose what finally made me make an appointment and actually say something is... lately, after I eat too much, I’ve been going upstairs and making myself throw up. And then I just come back and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed so no one notices. I just realized I need to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How often have you been making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, um... there has hardly been a week when I haven’t had at least one of those private eating spells. And then I always try to make up for it afterward. So that usually means throwing up, like I mentioned, or sometimes skipping meals, or just overdoing my walking to try and fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. Like I mentioned before about skipping meals... if I've had one of those spells, the next day I’ll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. Or, um, I'll push myself to do extra laps with my walking group. It's not because I feel energetic or want the fresh air, really. It's just because I’m trying to undo what I ate the night before.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. My weight has stayed pretty much the same over the last year. I suppose with the... the throwing up and the skipping meals I mentioned, it just sort of balances out, unfortunately. So, no, I haven't noticed any major changes up or down.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. Even though my daughter and a friend from church have both mentioned that I look too thin, I just... I panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug. Or if the scale goes up by just one pound, I feel like I've somehow become huge overnight. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, not really. If I have one of those episodes where I eat a lot, I always feel like I have to do something about it. I can't just... let it sit there. I always end up either making myself sick or fasting the next day. I don't think I've ever just eaten that much and done nothing afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, I really don't think I've ever done that. If I eat a large amount of food like that, I always feel that overwhelming need to fix it. I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period, but didn't try to make yourself throw up or skip meals afterwards?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that, but no. Like I said a moment ago, I've never done that. If I eat a lot of food at once, I always do something afterwards, whether that's making myself sick or skipping meals. I never just let it be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or anxious about it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. Honestly, it feels like we're going over the same thing again. The guilt and the anxiety are exactly why I do the things I just mentioned to you. I've never had a time where I ate like that and just felt fine or normal about it afterwards. The panic is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I suppose what finally made me make an appointment and actually say something is... lately, after I eat too much, I’ve been going upstairs and making myself throw up. And then I just come back and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed so no one notices. I just realized I need to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How often have you been making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, um... there has hardly been a week when I haven’t had at least one of those private eating spells. And then I always try to make up for it afterward. So that usually means throwing up, like I mentioned, or sometimes skipping meals, or just overdoing my walking to try and fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. Like I mentioned before about skipping meals... if I've had one of those spells, the next day I’ll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. Or, um, I'll push myself to do extra laps with my walking group. It's not because I feel energetic or want the fresh air, really. It's just because I’m trying to undo what I ate the night before.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. My weight has stayed pretty much the same over the last year. I suppose with the... the throwing up and the skipping meals I mentioned, it just sort of balances out, unfortunately. So, no, I haven't noticed any major changes up or down.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. Even though my daughter and a friend from church have both mentioned that I look too thin, I just... I panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug. Or if the scale goes up by just one pound, I feel like I've somehow become huge overnight. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, not really. If I have one of those episodes where I eat a lot, I always feel like I have to do something about it. I can't just... let it sit there. I always end up either making myself sick or fasting the next day. I don't think I've ever just eaten that much and done nothing afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, I really don't think I've ever done that. If I eat a large amount of food like that, I always feel that overwhelming need to fix it. I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period, but didn't try to make yourself throw up or skip meals afterwards?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that, but no. Like I said a moment ago, I've never done that. If I eat a lot of food at once, I always do something afterwards, whether that's making myself sick or skipping meals. I never just let it be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or anxious about it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. Honestly, it feels like we're going over the same thing again. The guilt and the anxiety are exactly why I do the things I just mentioned to you. I've never had a time where I ate like that and just felt fine or normal about it afterwards. The panic is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That's what starts the whole cycle I was just talking about. If no one is in the kitchen, I can just... go through all the leftovers from a church supper, plus crackers, cheese, cookies, whatever I baked for book club. I'll eat it all in one sitting. It's far, far more than I would ever serve myself at a normal meal.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, it's been pretty consistent. I'd say there's hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. And then, of course, I have to try and make up for it right after, whether that's by throwing up, just not eating the rest of the day, or pushing myself way too hard on my daily walks. It's at least a weekly thing, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be attractive if you gained a few pounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I would be attractive at all. It actually terrifies me. Even though my daughter and a woman from my church have both told me recently that I look too thin, I just... I can't see it that way. I still panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug, or if the scale goes up by just one pound. It feels like I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, and I just can't stand the thought of gaining any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Once I start, it's like a switch flips. It's a strange feeling... I'm almost watching myself keep eating, and there's a part of my brain saying, 'Linda, stop now, you've had enough.' But I just can't. My hands just keep reaching for more food until it's all gone. I really can't stop myself once it begins.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not usually after a normal amount of food. It's really just after I eat too much. In fact, that's what finally made me say something and decide to get help... After I overeat during one of those spells, I’ve been going upstairs and making myself throw up. Then I just come back down and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so my family doesn't realize what I've just done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: I suppose it must be. I mean... like I mentioned earlier about my daughter and the lady from church telling me I look too thin. I hear them say it, but when I look in the mirror, I just don't see what they see. I just see someone who needs to be really careful. And then I just come back and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed so no one notices. I just realized I need to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How often have you been making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, um... there has hardly been a week when I haven’t had at least one of those private eating spells. And then I always try to make up for it afterward. So that usually means throwing up, like I mentioned, or sometimes skipping meals, or just overdoing my walking to try and fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. Like I mentioned before about skipping meals... if I've had one of those spells, the next day I’ll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. Or, um, I'll push myself to do extra laps with my walking group. It's not because I feel energetic or want the fresh air, really. It's just because I’m trying to undo what I ate the night before.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. My weight has stayed pretty much the same over the last year. I suppose with the... the throwing up and the skipping meals I mentioned, it just sort of balances out, unfortunately. So, no, I haven't noticed any major changes up or down.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. Even though my daughter and a friend from church have both mentioned that I look too thin, I just... I panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug. Or if the scale goes up by just one pound, I feel like I've somehow become huge overnight. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, not really. If I have one of those episodes where I eat a lot, I always feel like I have to do something about it. I can't just... let it sit there. I always end up either making myself sick or fasting the next day. I don't think I've ever just eaten that much and done nothing afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, I really don't think I've ever done that. That's what starts the whole cycle I was just talking about. If no one is in the kitchen, I can just... go through all the leftovers from a church supper, plus crackers, cheese, cookies, whatever I baked for book club. I'll eat it all in one sitting. I'd say there's hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. It's at least a weekly thing, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be attractive if you gained a few pounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I would be attractive at all. And then I just come back and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed so no one notices. I just realized I need to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How often have you been making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, um... there has hardly been a week when I haven’t had at least one of those private eating spells. And then I always try to make up for it afterward. So that usually means throwing up, like I mentioned, or sometimes skipping meals, or just overdoing my walking to try and fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. Like I mentioned before about skipping meals... if I've had one of those spells, the next day I’ll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. Or, um, I'll push myself to do extra laps with my walking group. It's not because I feel energetic or want the fresh air, really. It's just because I’m trying to undo what I ate the night before.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. My weight has stayed pretty much the same over the last year. I suppose with the... the throwing up and the skipping meals I mentioned, it just sort of balances out, unfortunately. So, no, I haven't noticed any major changes up or down.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. Even though my daughter and a friend from church have both mentioned that I look too thin, I just... I panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug. Or if the scale goes up by just one pound, I feel like I've somehow become huge overnight. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, not really. If I have one of those episodes where I eat a lot, I always feel like I have to do something about it. I can't just... let it sit there. I always end up either making myself sick or fasting the next day. I don't think I've ever just eaten that much and done nothing afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, I really don't think I've ever done that. If I eat a large amount of food like that, I always feel that overwhelming need to fix it. I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period, but didn't try to make yourself throw up or skip meals afterwards?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that, but no. Like I said a moment ago, I've never done that. If I eat a lot of food at once, I always do something afterwards, whether that's making myself sick or skipping meals. I never just let it be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or anxious about it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. Honestly, it feels like we're going over the same thing again. The guilt and the anxiety are exactly why I do the things I just mentioned to you. I've never had a time where I ate like that and just felt fine or normal about it afterwards. The panic is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That's what starts the whole cycle I was just talking about. If no one is in the kitchen, I can just... go through all the leftovers from a church supper, plus crackers, cheese, cookies, whatever I baked for book club. I'll eat it all in one sitting. It's far, far more than I would ever serve myself at a normal meal.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, it's been pretty consistent. I'd say there's hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. And then, of course, I have to try and make up for it right after, whether that's by throwing up, just not eating the rest of the day, or pushing myself way too hard on my daily walks. It's at least a weekly thing, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be attractive if you gained a few pounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I would be attractive at all. I still panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug, or if the scale goes up by just one pound. It feels like I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, and I just can't stand the thought of gaining any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Once I start, it's like a switch flips. It's a strange feeling... I'm almost watching myself keep eating, and there's a part of my brain saying, 'Linda, stop now, you've had enough.' But I just can't. My hands just keep reaching for more food until it's all gone. I really can't stop myself once it begins.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not usually after a normal amount of food. It's really just after I eat too much. In fact, that's what finally made me say something and decide to get help... After I overeat during one of those spells, I’ve been going upstairs and making myself throw up. Then I just come back down and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so my family doesn't realize what I've just done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: I suppose it must be. I mean... like I mentioned earlier about my daughter and the lady from church telling me I look too thin. I hear them say it, but when I look in the mirror, I just don't see what they see. I don't think I've ever just... eaten all that food and left it at that. \n\nIf I have one of those spells where I lose control and eat a large amount, I always feel like I have to undo it. The guilt is just too heavy. So, no, I always end up going upstairs and making myself sick afterwards. I just realized I need to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How often have you been making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, um... there has hardly been a week when I haven’t had at least one of those private eating spells. And then I always try to make up for it afterward. Like I mentioned before about skipping meals... if I've had one of those spells, the next day I’ll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. It's just because I’m trying to undo what I ate the night before.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. My weight has stayed pretty much the same over the last year. I suppose with the... the throwing up and the skipping meals I mentioned, it just sort of balances out, unfortunately. Even though my daughter and a friend from church have both mentioned that I look too thin, I just... I panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug. Or if the scale goes up by just one pound, I feel like I've somehow become huge overnight. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, not really. If I have one of those episodes where I eat a lot, I always feel like I have to do something about it. I can't just... let it sit there. I always end up either making myself sick or fasting the next day. I don't think I've ever just eaten that much and done nothing afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, I really don't think I've ever done that. If I eat a large amount of food like that, I always feel that overwhelming need to fix it. I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period, but didn't try to make yourself throw up or skip meals afterwards?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that, but no. Like I said a moment ago, I've never done that. If I eat a lot of food at once, I always do something afterwards, whether that's making myself sick or skipping meals. I never just let it be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or anxious about it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. Honestly, it feels like we're going over the same thing again. The guilt and the anxiety are exactly why I do the things I just mentioned to you. I've never had a time where I ate like that and just felt fine or normal about it afterwards. The panic is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That's what starts the whole cycle I was just talking about. If no one is in the kitchen, I can just... go through all the leftovers from a church supper, plus crackers, cheese, cookies, whatever I baked for book club. I'll eat it all in one sitting. It's far, far more than I would ever serve myself at a normal meal.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, it's been pretty consistent. I'd say there's hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. And then, of course, I have to try and make up for it right after, whether that's by throwing up, just not eating the rest of the day, or pushing myself way too hard on my daily walks. It's at least a weekly thing, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be attractive if you gained a few pounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I would be attractive at all. It actually terrifies me. Even though my daughter and a woman from my church have both told me recently that I look too thin, I just... I can't see it that way. I still panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug, or if the scale goes up by just one pound. It feels like I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, and I just can't stand the thought of gaining any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Once I start, it's like a switch flips. It's a strange feeling... I'm almost watching myself keep eating, and there's a part of my brain saying, 'Linda, stop now, you've had enough.' But I just can't. My hands just keep reaching for more food until it's all gone. I really can't stop myself once it begins.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not usually after a normal amount of food. It's really just after I eat too much. In fact, that's what finally made me say something and decide to get help... After I overeat during one of those spells, I’ve been going upstairs and making myself throw up. Then I just come back down and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so my family doesn't realize what I've just done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: I suppose it must be. I mean... like I mentioned earlier about my daughter and the lady from church telling me I look too thin. I hear them say it, but when I look in the mirror, I just don't see what they see. I just see someone who needs to be really careful. So, yes, I guess my view of myself is pretty different from how they see me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to undo it afterwards, and if so, how often does that happen?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I've ever just... eaten all that food and left it at that. \n\nIf I have one of those spells where I lose control and eat a large amount, I always feel like I have to undo it. The guilt is just too heavy. So, no, I always end up going upstairs and making myself sick afterwards. I don't just sit with it.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up always after one of those episodes where you eat a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. I mean... I don't do it just out of the blue or after a regular dinner. It’s always tied to one of those times where I end up eating a really large amount of food. That's the only time I feel the need to go upstairs and make myself sick.\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a time when you make yourself throw up that isn't after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I just mentioned, it really is only after I eat a large amount. I don't ever... I wouldn't just go make myself sick after a normal lunch or something. Even though my daughter and a friend from church have both mentioned that I look too thin, I just... I panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug. Or if the scale goes up by just one pound, I feel like I've somehow become huge overnight. It really scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, not really. If I have one of those episodes where I eat a lot, I always feel like I have to do something about it. I can't just... let it sit there. I always end up either making myself sick or fasting the next day. I don't think I've ever just eaten that much and done nothing afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to undo it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, I really don't think I've ever done that. If I eat a large amount of food like that, I always feel that overwhelming need to fix it. I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period, but didn't try to make yourself throw up or skip meals afterwards?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that, but no. Like I said a moment ago, I've never done that. If I eat a lot of food at once, I always do something afterwards, whether that's making myself sick or skipping meals. I never just let it be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or anxious about it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. Honestly, it feels like we're going over the same thing again. The guilt and the anxiety are exactly why I do the things I just mentioned to you. I've never had a time where I ate like that and just felt fine or normal about it afterwards. The panic is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes. That's what starts the whole cycle I was just talking about. If no one is in the kitchen, I can just... go through all the leftovers from a church supper, plus crackers, cheese, cookies, whatever I baked for book club. I'll eat it all in one sitting. It's far, far more than I would ever serve myself at a normal meal.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, since last winter, it's been pretty consistent. I'd say there's hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. And then, of course, I have to try and make up for it right after, whether that's by throwing up, just not eating the rest of the day, or pushing myself way too hard on my daily walks. It's at least a weekly thing, sometimes more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be attractive if you gained a few pounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I would be attractive at all. It actually terrifies me. Even though my daughter and a woman from my church have both told me recently that I look too thin, I just... I can't see it that way. I still panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug, or if the scale goes up by just one pound. It feels like I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, and I just can't stand the thought of gaining any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Once I start, it's like a switch flips. It's a strange feeling... I'm almost watching myself keep eating, and there's a part of my brain saying, 'Linda, stop now, you've had enough.' But I just can't. My hands just keep reaching for more food until it's all gone. I really can't stop myself once it begins.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not usually after a normal amount of food. It's really just after I eat too much. In fact, that's what finally made me say something and decide to get help... After I overeat during one of those spells, I’ve been going upstairs and making myself throw up. Then I just come back down and act as if I’m just tidying the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so my family doesn't realize what I've just done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: I suppose it must be. I don't think I've ever just... eaten all that food and left it at that. \n\nIf I have one of those spells where I lose control and eat a large amount, I always feel like I have to undo it. The guilt is just too heavy. So, no, I always end up going upstairs and making myself sick afterwards. I don't do it just out of the blue or after a regular dinner. It’s always tied to one of those times where I end up eating a really large amount of food. That's the only time I feel the need to go upstairs and make myself sick.\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a time when you make yourself throw up that isn't after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. The panic is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes. If no one is in the kitchen, I can just... go through all the leftovers from a church supper, plus crackers, cheese, cookies, whatever I baked for book club. I'll eat it all in one sitting. I'd say there's hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... private eating spells. I still panic if my skirt feels even a little bit snug, or if the scale goes up by just one pound. It feels like I've somehow let myself become huge overnight, and I just can't stand the thought of gaining any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Once I start, it's like a switch flips. It's a strange feeling... I'm almost watching myself keep eating, and there's a part of my brain saying, 'Linda, stop now, you've had enough.' But I just can't. My hands just keep reaching for more food until it's all gone. I really can't stop myself once it begins.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not usually after a normal amount of food. It's really just after I eat too much. In fact, that's what finally made me say something and decide to get help... After I overeat during one of those spells, I’ve been going upstairs and making myself throw up. The guilt is just too heavy. So, no, I always end up going upstairs and making myself sick afterwards. I don't just sit with it.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up always after one of those episodes where you eat a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. I mean... I don't do it just out of the blue or after a regular dinner. It’s always tied to one of those times where I end up eating a really large amount of food. That's the only time I feel the need to go upstairs and make myself sick.\n\nDoctor: Is there ever a time when you make yourself throw up that isn't after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: No, not at all.
